金利上昇による企業の負担増加は 
いつ本格化するか

○ 日銀の政策金利や、金融市場で決まる国債金利に比べ、企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇はまだ
限定的。企業の新規借入金利や負債残高全体への影響波及に時間がかかることが背景 
○ 金融機関の新規貸出金利は、概ね3カ月かけて政策金利の変化を反映する傾向。特に地域金融機関
で上昇がやや遅く、企業規模により有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが異なる一因に 
○ 借入・社債金利の動きを踏まえると、企業の利払い負担増加は2026～2028年度に本格化し、年間
約3兆円の利益下押し要因に。これまで以上に資本効率を意識した経営が求められる

１．政策金利や国債金利が上昇するも、企業の利払い負担増加はまだ本格化せず 
日本銀行は2026年9月の金融政策決定会合で、 政策金利を1.0％から1.25％へ引き上げた。これは1995
年以来、31年ぶりの高水準である1。また、金融市場で決まる10年国債金利（いわゆる長期金利）は、
2026年9月に節目となる3％を超えた。こちらも1996年以来30年ぶりの高さであり、政策金利や国債金
利はまさに歴史的な転換点を迎えている。 
一方で、 企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇は今のところ限定的だ。 図表1は、 企業の有利子負債利子
率 （＝支払利息等÷有利子負債残高） を指標金利 （政策金利、10年国債金利）と比較したものである。

### Page 2

2

図中の凡例にある数字は、 各金利についてマイナス金利政策解除 （2024年3月19日 ）前 の2023年10～12
月期と、直近の2026年4～6月期を比較した値だ。政策金利が＋0.93％Pt、10年国債金利が＋1.80％Pt
と大きく上昇した一方で、企業の有利子負債利子率は＋0.42％Ptと上昇幅が小さく、企業の利払い負
担の増加はまだ本格化していない。実際、日銀短観における企業の資金繰りに関する調査（図表2）を
みると、「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」の回答率を差し引いたDIが、2021年以降プラス圏で概
ね横ばいを維持している。企業の実感としても、これまでに実施された複数回の利上げや、金融市場
における国債金利の大幅な上昇は、まだ資金調達環境の悪化につながっていないようだ。 
なぜ、こうしたギャップが生じるのか。主な要因として考えられるのは次の2点である。 
第一の要因は、指標金利と企業の新規調達金利とのタイムラグだ。政策金利などの指標金利が変化
してから、企業が新たに資金を調達する際の金利が変化するまで、少し時間がかかる。特に、企業の
有利子負債の半分強を占める金融機関からの借入において（図表3）、新規調達金利のタイムラグが生
じやすいと考えられる。 
第二の要因は、企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグだ。企業が
新たに資金調達する際の金利が上昇しても、それが有利子負債残高全体にすぐ反映されるわけではな
い。 企業の金融機関借入金の4～5割程度が固定金利型であるほか2、 主に大企業が発行する社債の多く
が固定利付債であり、過去の低金利時代に調達した負債がまだ残っているためだ。 
本稿では、 これら2つの要因を踏まえて、政策金利・国債金利などの指標金利上昇を受けた企業の利
払い負担増加が、いつ本格的に現れるかを試算する3。 
２．金融機関からの新規借入金利は、地域金融機関で上昇ペースが遅い傾向 
まず、第一の要因（指標金利から企業の新規調達金利へのタイムラグ）の影響を検証しよう。図表
3に示したように、 企業の有利子負債には金融機関などからの借入金と、 主に大企業が発行する社債が
ある。このうち、社債を新規発行する際の金利は概ね国債金利に連動して決まり、タイムラグがほぼ
図表 3 企業の有利子負債残高の内訳 図表 4 金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利

含まれるが、 シェアが小さい （0.1％程度） ため表示し
ていない。

### Page 3

3

無いため、ここでの検証は金融機関などから新規に借り入れる際の金利が対象になる。 
図表4は、金融機関の新規貸出金利（金融機関からみた貸出）を、業態別に示したものだ。都市銀行
では新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが速い一方、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では政策金利の上昇に比べて新
規貸出金利の上昇ペースがやや遅い。こうした地域金融機関における金利上昇の遅れが、指標金利と
企業の新規調達金利のタイムラグに影響していると考えられる。 
金融機関の業態によって新規貸出金利の上昇ペースに差があるのは、貸出の金利形態別シェアが異
なることが一因であろう。大手行では企業向け貸出全体の約6割を占める変動金利型貸出のうち、ほと
んどが「市場金利連動」であるため、金融市場の変化による影響がすぐに反映されやすい。他方で、
地方銀行や信用金庫では変動金利型貸出のうち「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが高い。短期プ
ライムレートとは、各金融機関が設定する企業向け貸出の最優遇金利である。政策金利の変更などを
反映して決められることが多いが、各金融機関の競争環境などによって反映時期・度合いに差が生じ
る4。これにより、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では都市銀行に比べて新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが遅くな
る傾向があると考えられる5。 
こうした金融機関業態別の違いは、企業サイドにおいて、有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが規模別
に異なる一因になっている可能性がある。足元の金利上昇局面では、都市銀行からの借入が多いと考
えられる大企業（資本金10億円以上）で有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが速く、信用金庫など地域金
融機関からの借入が多いと考えられる小規模企業（同1千万円未満）で上昇ペースが遅い（図表5）。
2010年代の金利低下局面では大企業の有利子負債利子率が小規模企業を下回る傾向にあったが、足元
では完全に逆転している。大企業は社債が有利子負債残高の約3分の1を占めるため、金融機関の新規
貸出金利のみが原因とは言えないものの、金融機関業態別の違いが企業規模別の資金調達環境に影響
を及ぼしている可能性がある点は興味深い。 
では、今後日銀がさらに利上げを進めていくと、金融機関の新規貸出金利はどの程度のペースで、
どこまで上昇していくか。過去の政策金利と新規貸出金利の関係を統計的に分析すると、 政策金利1％

平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業

### Page 4

4

Ptの上昇は新規貸出金利を当月に0.31％Pt押し上げ、その後さらに3カ月間かけて当月からの累計で
0.85％Pt押し上げる傾向があることが明らかになった。日銀が今後2027 年半ばにかけて政策金利を
2.0％まで引き上げていくことを前提にすると6、金融機関の新規貸出金利（2026年8月時点で1.78％）
は2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度へ上昇すると試算される （図表6） 。 マイナス金利政策の解除前と
比較すると、2％Pt弱の上昇になる計算だ。 
３．企業の利払い負担増加は今後 2028 年度にかけて本格化。 利益を年間約 3 兆円下押し 
次に、第二の要因（企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグ）の影
響を検証しよう。金融機関の新規貸出金利や社債の新規発行金利の上昇が、それぞれの残高全体にど
の程度のペースで反映されるかを確認し、企業の利払い負担がいつ本格化に増加するか試算する。 
金融機関の新規貸出金利と貸出残高全体の金利を比較すると、今回の金利上昇局面では新規貸出金
の上昇に対して貸出金利残高全体の金利上昇がやや遅れている（図表7）。 先に述べたように、金融機
関の貸出の一部が固定金利型であり、新規貸出や借り換えに伴って新規貸出金利の上昇が残高全体へ
波及するのに時間がかかるためだ。 
両者の過去の関係を統計的に分析すると、新規貸出金利の上昇がおよそ5年程度かけて残高全体の金
利を緩やかに押し上げる傾向があるとの結果になった。 図表6で試算したように、 先行きの新規貸出金
利が2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度まで上昇していくと想定すると、貸出残高全体の金利（2026年
8月時点で1.51％ ）は2027年末に2.0％程度、2028年末に2.3％程度と緩やかに上昇し、2030年代前半に
は新規貸出金利と同じ2.65％程度へ収束していく計算になる。 
同様に社債について、 新発債金利と既発債金利を比較したものが図表8だ。 新発債金利は2022年ごろ
から上昇して足元では2％台後半に達しているが、既発債金利はまだ1％強にとどまっており、金融機
関の貸出金利に比べると残高全体への反映に時間がかかりやすいことがうかがえる。なお、日本で新

### Page 5

5

規に発行される社債の平均年限は現在5～6年であり、新発債金利は概ね5 年国債金利に連動して動く
傾向があることから、先に述べたように指標金利→新発債金利のタイムラグはほとんど生じない。 
今後、社債残高が物価上昇にあわせて年間＋2％のペースで増加していくとの前提を置き、 先行きの
償還に伴う借り換えや追加的な新規発行が行われていく場合、既発債金利は2030年代初めにかけて年
間＋0.3％Pt程度の緩やかなペースで上昇していくと試算される（新発債金利は5年国債金利の先行き
予測値に連動すると想定）。7年債や10年債といった年限が長めの社債も一定の規模があることから、
新発債金利の上昇が既発債金利に反映されるまでに時間を要する結果になった。 
最後に、金融機関の貸出金利（企業からみた借入金利7）と社債金利の試算結果を用いて、企業の有
利子負債利回りへの影響を検証する。企業の有利子負債に占める借入と社債の金額シェアを先行き一
定として、 貸出金利と社債金利の変化を有利子負債利子率に反映したものが図表9だ。2023～2025年度
の有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースは年間＋0.1～＋0.2％Pt程度と緩やかだったが、2026～2028年度に
は同＋0.3～＋0.4％Ptに加速すると見込まれる。企業の有利子負債の多くを占める借入金の金利上昇
が、主な押し上げ要因だ。2029年以後は借入金の金利上昇が落ち着く一方、社債金利の上昇継続を受
け、有利子負債利子率は2030年代にかけて緩やかに3％程度の水準まで上昇していくだろう。 
このように、金利上昇による企業の利払い負担増加は今後2028年度にかけて本格化していく見込み
だ。企業全体の有利子負債残高が先行き年間＋2％のペースで拡大していくと仮定すると、 支払利息等
が年間約3兆円増加する計算であり、経常利益（金融・保険を除くベースで2025年度：124.8兆円）に
対し年間2.4％の押し下げ要因に相当する8。 
今後、有利子負債利子率が上昇していく過程では、それに見合った利益率を達成できるかが問われ
るだろう。2025年度時点では投下資本利益率（ROIC、3.70％）の有利子負債利子率（1.45％）に対す
るスプレッドが2％Ptを上回っており、ROICでみた企業の資本効率は外部資金調達コストを十分カバー
できている（図表10）9。しかし、 有利子負債利子率が今後3％程度へ上昇していくと、ROICが現在の水
準のままではスプレッドがコロナ禍初期の2020年度並みに縮小し、企業の利払い負担に対する利益の
図表 9 企業の有利子負債利子率の試算 図表 10 有利子負債利子率と投下資本利益率

（注） 有利子負債利子率の対象は、金融 ・ 保険を除く全産業 ・
全規模。先行きは、法人企業統計調査における借入金
残高と社債残高のシェアを一定として、有利子負債利
子率への影響を試算

余力が弱まってしまう。日本の金利上昇が一段と進み、企業の利払い負担が本格的に増加していくと

［参考文献］ 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、 投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」、2026年10月2日

1 現在の政策金利は無担保コール翌日物金利の誘導目標だが、1995 年時点では公定歩合を政策金利と見なして時系列比較してお
り、厳密には政策金利の意味するものが異なる。 
2 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2026年4 月）の図表Ⅲ-1-12 によれば、法人向け貸出のうち、大手行で約40％、地域銀
行（地方銀行、第二地方銀行）や信用金庫で 45～50％程度が固定金利貸出である（2025 年 9 月末時点）。 
3 金利上昇による企業への影響には、利払いの増加によるデメリットだけでなく、利子収入の増加によるメリットもある。本稿
では、そのうちデメリットの面だけに焦点を当てて分析しており、メリットについては稿を改めて論じたい。 
4 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2025年4 月）の図表Ⅵ-1-8 では、地域銀行を対象に、2024 年7 月の政策金利上昇を受け
た短期プライムレートの引き上げタイミング、引き上げ幅、引き上げ後金利の貸出先適用割合について分析が行われている。 
5 図表 4 に示した金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利には、企業向け以外の貸出も含まれる。したがって、企業向け貸出における
「市場金利連動」 、 「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが業態別に異なることだけでなく、貸出全体に占める住宅ローン貸出
のシェアが業態別に異なることなども、新規貸出金利の上昇ペースの違いに影響していると考えられる。 
6 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
7 金融機関の貸出金利には企業向け以外の貸出も含まれるが、ここでは簡易的に、企業向け貸出金利が貸出全体の金利に連動す
ると仮定して試算した。 
8 本稿では金利上昇による利払いの増加のみを計算対象としているため、利子収入の増加も考慮すれば経常利益への下押し圧力
は緩和すると考えられる。 
9 投下資本利益率（ROIC）と本来比較すべきは、負債だけでなく自己資本のコストも考慮した加重平均資本コスト（WACC）だ
が、本稿では金利上昇の影響を直接評価する観点から、投下資本利益率と有利子負債利子率を比べている。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 14 日

数据点评  
生产与消费 价格修复分化 ，内需 回暖 尚有 “时间差 ” 
——2026 年 9 月通胀数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：CPI方面，9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，涨幅较上月扩大0.1个百分点，连
续两个月回升，但低于市场预期。食品价格同比降幅扩大至1.7%，主要受鲜
菜、 鲜果价格下跌影响， 猪价同比跌幅收窄； 非食品价格同比涨幅扩大至1.5%，
主要受国际油价反弹及电子产品价格上涨带动，其中汽油价格同比上涨
16.6%，拉动整体 CPI同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点。环比看，CPI 上涨 0.2%，
涨幅较上月收窄 0.2 个百分点，核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平于 1.0%，服务价格环
比转跌，反映国内终端需求修复仍偏温和。展望10 月，国际油价高位运行及
AI 算力需求带动的消费电子涨价仍将提供支撑， 但蔬果价格同比降幅扩大、 猪
肉价格环比转跌，叠加服务价格季节性回落，CPI 整体或延续温和修复。PPI
方面，9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%， 涨幅较上月扩大1.2个百分点， 高于市场预期，
主要受国际油价反弹及低基数效应推动。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业、 石油
和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大至
35.3%、24.5%和 21.6%，化工、化纤等行业价格亦明显回升，反映能源价格
上涨正沿产业链向中游原材料环节传导；电气机械、 计算机通信及其他电子设
备制造业价格环比均上涨0.4%，AI 算力相关需求形成一定支撑。 环比看，PPI
上涨 1.2%，涨幅较上月扩大 0.8 个百分点，石油相关行业价格上涨是主要拉
动。展望后续，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势、国际油价高位运行、冬季
煤炭备货及低基数效应仍将支撑PPI， 但上下游价格传导不畅、 终端消费偏弱，
生活资料价格上涨空间仍然有限，PPI 修复或延续结构性分化。 
 输入性因素支撑 CPI 同比修复，核心 CPI 保持稳定。同比来看，9 月 CPI 同
比涨幅扩大 0.1个百分点至 0.9%，连续两个月回升，增速低于wind一致预期
（1.02%）。其中，食品价格同比下降1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3个百分点。非食品
价格同比涨幅扩大 0.3 个百分点至 1.5%，主要受能源及电子产品价格涨幅扩
大影响。9 月中东地缘冲突持续发酵， 能源供应风险上升， 国际油价二次反弹，
带动国内能源价格涨幅扩大，对整体CPI 形成支撑。环比来看，9 月 CPI环比
涨幅收窄 0.2个百分点至 0.2%， 其中食品价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现，
非食品价格环比涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.2%。9 月核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平
于 1%， 核心CPI 环比回落至 0%。核心 CPI 连续数月运行在 1.0%附近， 说明
剔除输入性扰动后，国内终端需求的修复力度仍然温和。展望 10 月，国际油
价高位运行及 AI算力需求带动的消费电子价格上涨仍将为CPI提供支撑，但
食品价格仍面临一定下行压力。 同时， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶
段，核心 CPI 修复力度预计仍偏温和。整体来看，输入性因素仍将对CPI 形
成支撑，但内需修复偏缓或限制涨幅，预计10 月 CPI 同比延续温和回升。 
 食品价格同比降幅扩大，蔬果价格下跌是主要拖累。同比来看，9 月食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3 个百分点，主要受鲜菜、鲜果价格下跌及猪肉
价格仍处低位影响。 食品细项中，鲜菜、鲜果价格同比分别下降8.1%、1.5%，
降幅均扩大，反映季节性供给增加对农产品价格形成压制。牛肉、 羊肉价格同
比分别上升 7.5%、6.4%，涨幅均扩大。猪价同比下降 10.0%，降幅收窄 1.8
个百分点，价格弱势有所缓解，但同比仍处于较大跌幅区间。环比来看，食品
价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现。食品细项中，鲜菜价格环比涨幅收窄5
个百分点至 0.5%，鲜果价格环比由下降转为上升 0.6%。猪价环比涨幅收窄
0.1 个百分点至 1.2%，9 月猪肉均价同比下降 13.58%，降幅继续收窄。从最
新的高频数据走势看，截至 2026年 10 月 13 日，同比方面，10 月初 28 种重

转为下降 2.1%。服务 CPI 环比由上升转为下降 0.2%。服务中， 随着暑期结束，
飞机票、 宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格分别下降8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%； 受成本走
高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格分别上涨 3.7%、
2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%。展望 10 月，非食品价格预计延续分化走势，同比仍受
到能源价格回升和电子产品涨价支撑， 但环比上涨动能可能进一步减弱。 一方

面， 国际油价同比改善以及电子产品价格上涨仍将支撑非食品价格表现； 另一
方面， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶段， 叠加黄金等前期涨价品类涨
幅收窄，非食品价格环比或继续放缓。
 油价反弹带动 PPI超预期回升，资源品价格韧性较强。从同比看，受国际油价
涨幅回升及低基数效应影响，PPI同比涨幅扩大 1.2 个百分点至 5.0%，增速高
于 wind 一致预期 （4.3%）。生产资料同比涨幅扩大1.5 个百分点至 6.5%，生
活资料同比下降 0.1%，降幅收窄 0.4 个百分点。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选
业同比涨幅扩大 8.7个百分点至 35.3%，主要受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、 主产
矿区供应阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响。石油和天然气开采
业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大14.0、10.5 个百分点
至 24.5%和 21.6%。化学纤维制造业、 化学原料及化学制品制造业同比涨幅分
别扩大至 12.3%和 12.1%。一方面， 国际原油价格上涨直接推升采掘和炼化环

节价格；另一方面，石油作为基础原材料，通过燃料、化工品等渠道向中下游
传导， 带动化学原料、 化纤等行业价格上涨。从环比看，PPI环比涨幅扩大 0.8
个百分点至 1.2%， 生产资料环比涨幅扩大 1 个百分点至 1.4%， 生活资料环比
涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.1%。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业环比涨幅扩大
6.7 个百分点至 9.5%；石油相关行业价格上涨动能显著增强。 石油和天然气开
采业、 石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格环比涨幅分别扩大4.3、4.9个百分点至
10.4%和 8.4%。电气机械和器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业
价格环比均上涨 0.4%，AI 与算电协同带来的结构性需求推动资本开支扩张。
国际层面，10 月 13 日布伦特原油现货价格为130.92美元/桶，10 月初油价同
比涨幅扩大 28.36 个百分点至 100.08%。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡
的紧张局势持续升温，伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制，美国持续强化对伊朗石
油贸易的封锁，区域供应风险上升。原油市场供给担忧升温，国际油价或维持

高位，并通过能源、化工等产业链向国内工业品价格传导。9 月，PPI 同比涨
幅（5.0%）与 CPI 同比涨幅（0.9%）之间的差距达到 4.1 个百分点，工厂端
的原材料和能源成本正在快速上涨，但尚未同步传导至终端消费市场，中下游
企业的利润空间正受到挤压。后续来看，国际油价高位震荡、冬季煤炭备货叠
加低基数构成后续 PPI 的核心推升力量。石油与煤炭开采价格同比或继续走
阔，并向化工、化纤等中下游传导；AI 算力相关电气设备、通信设备制造价
格或维持增长。 但上下游传导仍不畅， 终端消费偏弱或仍制约生活资料涨价空
间。
 风险提示：国内政策落地节奏不及预期，国际大宗商品价格波动超预期， 海外
地缘扰动风险。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规 渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

南亞科(2408)

投資評等 目標價

前次投資建議：2026.07.13： 
「買進」 ，目標價: $710

報告日期：2026.10.13 研 究 員：王穎

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好記憶體需求緊俏有利報價走升，維持「買進」 投資評等，目標價 800 元(6 x 27(F)PER)，
潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/12 收盤價： 527.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 
611.7%，毛利率 85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利
益為 1068.9億元 ， 業外收入24.38億 ， 稅後淨利為 876.3
億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營收成
長及毛利雙雙成長受惠 平均銷售單價季增超過 六十位數
百分比，而季減中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分
比。 
➢ 展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，
且南亞科 AI相關產品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載
出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價將持續於 26Q4上
漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 
88.1%，EPS為 30.35 元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於
DRAM產能持續滿載，預期 DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產
品將持續供不應求。1C 產品預期於 27Q1 開始驗證，有
利打入 AI伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此
兆豐預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，
毛利率 83.3/86.3%，EPS 為 77.85/133.2 元。南亞科目
前位於(3.9 X 27(F) PER)，看好記憶體需求緊俏報價持續
走升， 維持「買進」投資評等，目標價  800 元(6 x 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

個股報告 
26Q3 ASP成長帶動營收毛利雙雙上升 
26Q3 營收  1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 611.7%，毛利率
85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利益為 1068.9 億元，業外收入 24.38
億，稅後淨利為 876.3 億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營
收成長及毛利雙雙成長受惠平均銷售單價季增超過六十位數百分比，而季減
中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分比。

26Q4合約價持續上漲  
展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，且南亞科 AI 相關產
品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價
將持續於 26Q4 上漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 88.1%，EPS 為 30.35
元。

- 4 - 
個股報告

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Takeaways on Photonics from
2026 OCP Global Summit
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA

Andy.Meng@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7689
Betty Chen

Betty.H.Chen@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7213
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line

On Day 3 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, there is a full-day special focus on
Photonics. Industry participants such as NVIDIA, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle, Broadcom,
Celestica, Arista, Ciena, Amphenol, Lumentum, and Huawei presented their views.
We summarize the key findings below:
1) When the moderator asks key industry players whether they will apply CPO or
NPO, almost all companies answered 'Both'.
2) AI density depends on optical connectivity. With the day's focus on Photonics, the

importance of optical connectivity was well highlighted in each session.
3) Five key metrics for new optical technology: 1) performance, 2) power, 3) cost, 4)
speed to deployment and 5) reliability and serviceability. Huawei believes NPO has
advantages on maintainability, open decoupling, availability and industry
implementation.
4) Scale-in is another new approach to enhance AI density.
5) Root cause of CPO failures: 83-85% of failures due to ELS, 11-17% due to fiber/

connector. Dust and dirt are also common failure issues.
6) Optics vs. Copper: Full Optical Pod is targeted towards scale-up sizes >576
accelerators, Optics and Copper to coexist between 72 and 576 accelerators.
Greater China Technology Hardware: Takeaways on NPO from 2026 OCP Global
Summit (14 Oct 2026)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
A full day discussion of Photonics further demonstrated the importance of
optical connectivity.
October 15, 2026 10:04 PM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Genius Electronic Optical
Core resilience with CPO upside optionality
Raise PT to NT$1,250 on potential CPO contribution and intact core business
We reiterate Buy and raise our  price target to NT$1,250 from NT$700, rolling valuation
to 2028E and moving from P/B to a P/E-based SOTP that separately values the core lens
business and the emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) business. We believe Genius is
making progress sampling three precision components inside the fiber array unit (FAU)

targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.  We see potential for the company
to qualify the leading FAU module supplier into NVIDIA's CPO program. At the same
time, its core business is outperforming Largan, growing +1% YoY in Q326 vs. -11%
YoY for Largan as its share rises on periscope lens. Compared with the pressure on
Largan's margins from green electricity prices, we believe Genius should maintain its
margin, as its main production site is in Xiamen, whereas Largan's production in Taiwan

is affected by higher electricity prices.
Core intact: periscope share gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We still keep 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.
CPO component strategy offers a different route to market from Largan
Unlike Largan's pursuit of complete FAU assembly, Genius is targeting high precision
optical components within the module, including: 1) high-precision prism to collimate
light from the PIC into the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place,
and 3) MT ferrules (high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). We

believe Genius' customer is one of the key module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27 program
targeting over 50% share. Our base case models a complete-FAU market of US$1.85bn
in 2028E and US$4.45bn in 2030E, with Genius base case for US$185m and US$668m
representing 10% and 15% revenue share of the complete-FAU revenue TAM.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with  PT lifted to NT$1,250
We fine-tune our 2026/2027 basic EPS estimates from NT$37.2/NT$41.5 to NT$36.7/NT

$44.0 and introduce 2028E EPS of NT$49.25. We move from P/B to P/E SOTP as the core
business now delivers steadier earnings and CPO warrants separate valuation. Our NT
$1,250 PT comprises NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) and NT$505
for CPO (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E). Catalysts include qualifications at
leading FAU makers and the board approved 7mn share private private placement with
potential strategic investor (albeit 6% dilution if issued). Our US$1.8bn CPO value

compares to US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.

### Page 2

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Could Genius' CPO products contribute meaningful revenue in a few years?
Yes, from H227E into 2028E. We see Genius' CPO strategy as feasible and lower-risk than Largan's.
Largan's goal is to supply the complete FAU; Genius is entering at the component level, supplying V-
grooves, MT ferrules and prisms to FAU makers. This lets Genius sell into multiple FAU designs and

integrators, limiting its downside if the market converges on a single winning architecture.
Management believes multi-sourcing is inevitable once volumes scale and has seen early indications
of potentially substantial demand from US optical players, including Corning and Coherent. Genius
has a partnership with FOCI and is pursuing other FAU integrators including we believe Coherent;
management suggested further updates could come in the near term. Some parts already ship in

small volumes, but we do not expect meaningful revenue until H227 or 2028.
Q: Will Genius benefit from further lens technology upgrades in the coming years?
Yes. We expect premium lens participation and product mix to support core revenue growth of 8.0%,
9.7% and 9.1% in 2026-28E. We believe Genius is gaining share from Largan in Apple's periscope
lens supply chain after initially lagging. Apple (70–80% of Genius' sales) sell-through units grew 9%

YoY YTD through August according to Counterpoint, and Genius' 9M26 revenue grew 10% YoY on
a similar trajectory. Largan grew only 2% in 9M26, with Q326 down 11% YoY. For the iPhone 18
series, Genius participates in every lens spec except the variable-aperture upgrade, where it stepped
back because of pricing pressure from Chinese competitors. Management says Genius has the
technical capability and could enter if future specs or design changes improve the opportunity.

Genius offers a combination of an established earnings base from smartphone lens and potential
exposure to the passive components required for optical interconnects. We remain constructive on
the lens business and see CPO as a potentially material addition from 2028. The component strategy
provides several routes to customers, but the valuation should reflect uncertainty over qualified
content, allocation and margins. Our Buy rating and NT$1,250 PT is based  on NT$745 base business

(15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505 for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in
2028E). The CPO option for Genius is US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.
Genius share gains are showing in reported sales, with YTD sales +10% YoY vs. +2% YoY for Largan.
The company is also participating in every spec except variable aperture.  For CPO, Genius delivered
samples of its prism, V-groove, and MT ferrule to FOCI at the end of May, with initial feedback broadly

on track and no major issues reported to date. Our checks indicate the company is also positioning to
supply one of the leading FAU module suppliers for NVIDIA's CPO program from H227.
At NT$1,060, the shares trade at 24.1x our 2027E core EPS and 21.5x our 2028E core EPS, factoring
some of the CPO opportunity in the multiple rather than in estimates relative to the 10 year average
17x P/E. Applying a 15x valuation to the smartphone lens business + 40x to the CPO option from our

scenario of NT$9.30 EPS in 2028E yields upside from the last close at NT$1,060 to a NT$1,250 share
price.

### Page 4

Genius position in the CPO value chain
Genius is targeting the CPO market as a component supplier while Largan is advancing
from components into full-FAU assembly. Genius is sampling three components for CPO
with FAU makers, including: 1) high-precision prism to redirect light from the PIC into
the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place, and 3) MT ferrules
(high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). The companies overlap in

capability, with Largan also having PMLA and fiber arrays but not getting into some
components like MT ferrules and V-Grooves.
Genius's optical design, precision tooling, metrology and volume-manufacturing
experience offer it a good entry point for this market. Commercial differentiation will
depend on reproducible optical performance, dimensional tolerances, thermal stability
and customer yield.  It has announced collaboration with FOCI and  we believe has

potential to also work with one of NVIDIA's leading module suppliers for its H227
program.

Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes an even mix of
these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030E
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028E, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T
switch ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030E, 55% of 1.1mn

204.8T switch ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030E, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028E and 8.9mn in 2030E.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030E
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native
NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028E and 35.6mn in 2030E. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn

market in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028E; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030E). The expansion to 2030E comes from wider optical
adoption and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline
from US$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Genius CPO opportunity

### Page 7

We assume in base case Genius captures 10% of the TAM in 2028E and 15% by 2030E.
The base case in 2028E is consistent with US$30 of prism, V-groove and ferrule content
in half of all FAUs or US$15 average content spread across the FAU market.  The
2030E bull case (25%) or US$37.50 would require near full content of some of the sub-
components or otherwise ability to integrate more of the solution value by that time.
Our bear case assumes only 1% share of the opportunity in 2028E and 5% of the

opportunity in 2030E, with EPS even by 2030E falling to NT$4 and yielding limited
valuation potential for the shares.

Reiterate Buy and raise PT to NT$1,250 valuing in CPO optionality

We adjust our valuation method from P/B to P/E SOTP to better reflect the company’s
core business and its opportunity within CPO industry while the core smartphone
business is demonstrating more stable earnings to use P/E vs. prior P/B. Our PT at NT
$1,250 is based on NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505
for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E) with catalysts from
upcoming qualifications into leading FAU makers and potential strategic private

placement with board approving 7mn share private placement (albeit 6% dilution if
issued). We note Genius CPO option valued at US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our
Largan valuation.
Our price target uses 2028 earnings for both businesses, reflecting the modeled start of
meaningful CPO contribution. We apply 15x core EPS of NT$49.25 and 40x CPO EPS of
NT$12.72 for a NT$1,250 price target. The 40x multiple assumes the optical business
can sustain strong growth after its initial ramp; qualification, concentration and margin

risk limit the confidence that can be placed on that earnings stream.
In the upside case, stronger premium lens participation and manufacturing execution lift
2028E core EPS to NT$54.32. Faster optical adoption expands the 2028E FAU market to
US$3.46bn; 15% effective revenue capture and a 31% pretax margin produce
approximately NT$36.88 CPO EPS. Applying 15x to core earnings and 42x to CPO
earnings gives approximately NT$2,365.
In the downside case, weaker lens demand and margin pressure lower core EPS to NT

$44.29. Slower optical adoption leaves a US$0.46bn FAU market, of which Genius
captures 1%, producing only NT$0.29 CPO EPS. Applying 12x and 38x gives
approximately NT$540. The downside is driven by both a weaker core business and a
much smaller optical contribution.

Upside/Downside Spectrum
Upside/Downside Spectrum
Genius (3406.TW) trading at 1,060 at closed Oct 8.
Source: UBS estimates
UPSIDE (NT$2,365): Lens upgrades accelerate and Genius gains share in higher-end
specs. 2027E/28E sales grow 11.8%/9.1% YoY (base 9.7%/9.1%), and GM expands to
35.6%/35.5% (base 34.4%/34.3%) on better yields and mix. 2028E core EPS of NT
$54.3 (+10% vs base) at 15x gives NT$815. Faster CPO adoption and stronger AI capex

lift the 2028E FAU TAM to US$3.5bn (19.8mn units at US$175); at 15% capture, CPO
sales reach US$519mn and CPO EPS NT$36.9, worth NT$1,550 at 42x.
BASE (NT$1,250):  Core NT$745 (15x NT$49.25) plus CPO NT$505 (40x NT$12.72), as
above.
DOWNSIDE (NT540): Smartphone demand weakens, legacy price erosion continues
and share gains stall. 2027E sales grow 7.4% YoY with GM of 33.4%, and 2028E core
EPS falls to NT$44.3 (−10% vs base), worth NT$530 at 12x. CPO adoption is slow

(10%/3% CPO/NPO in scale-out, no ASIC attach), leaving a US$0.46bn TAM; at 1%
capture, CPO is worth NT$11.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our Genius price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model. Genius' growth is
highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by volatility in handset
demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their camera design.

Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of new
technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business. We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from

smartphone OEM clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a
slower-thanexpected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success
below the market's elevated expectations. We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-
expected CPO qualification process at Largan. In addition, if the company demonstrates
exceptionally efficient and highly accurate automation capabilities, it could support greater
market share gains within the industry than we currently anticipate.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

Document 271: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Rate decisions in Hungary, Turkey and Kazakhstan

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Rate decisions in Hungary, Turkey and Kazakhstan.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4

### Page 1

Hungary, Turkey and Kazakhstan will announce interest rate
decisions next week, while Poland releases industrial output,
labour market and retail sales data

Adam Antoniak, Peter Virovacz, Muhammet Mercan and Dmitry
Dolgin
Falling inﬂation has markets increasingly betting on a Turkish rate cut
Poland: Industrial output and labour market
data in focus
September industrial output and labour market data will be
released on Tuesday. Although the European and German
economies show signs of recovery, Polish manufacturing remains
fragile and uneven. Some sectors, including electrical equipment,
machinery and equipment, and other transport equipment, are
performing relatively well. Others, such as textiles, furniture and the
automotive industry, continue to struggle. With the calendar eﬀect
neutral (the number of working days is the same as a year earlier)
and a high comparison base, we forecast low single-digit growth in
industrial production in September. At the same time, we expect PPI

### Page 2

inﬂation to accelerate further, driven mainly by higher crude oil
reﬁning costs.
Poland’s labour market remains stable. It is no longer exceptionally
tight, nor showing signs of weakening. Wage growth in the
enterprise sector has moderated and is stabilising at around 6.0%,
no longer exerting signiﬁcant pressure on price growth.
Employment continues to decline amid an ageing population and
an insuﬃcient inﬂow of younger workers to replace those retiring,

particularly in manufacturing. Enterprise-sector data do not
capture the employment of immigrant workers.
Thursday brings the release of September retail sales data. Despite
some moderation, private consumption appears relatively resilient
to the current energy shock and high retail fuel prices. To some
extent, the impact of rising fuel prices on household ﬁnances is
being cushioned by government measures temporarily reducing
excise duty and VAT on fuels. Demand for durable goods remains

solid, while some restraint is visible in day-to-day spending on
necessities. Given the substantial savings accumulated in previous
quarters, households should be able to smooth their consumption
over time. Nevertheless, household consumption growth this year
is projected to be slower than in 2025.
Hungary: NBH expected to keep rates
unchanged
The National Bank of Hungary’s watchlist is fairly limited to ﬁscal
policy and risk sentiment. In practice, however, it is diﬃcult to

assess these factors in the run-up to the October rate-setting
meeting on Tuesday. Although inﬂation ﬁgures were dovish,
market volatility in relation to geopolitical developments remains
high. With no clear de-escalation in sight, risk sentiment won't
allow the NBH to cut rates now.
Regarding ﬁscal policy, although the central bank is aware of the
2027 budget, as the NBH governor sits on the Fiscal Council that
reviews and approves it, no public information will be available on

this. Therefore, we do not believe this will provide an opportunity for
immediate monetary policy easing. Given the timing, we anticipate

another on-hold meeting. However, if the ﬁscal situation were to
materially reduce the country-speciﬁc risk premium, we wouldn't
rule out the possibility of a rate cut in November. Nevertheless, the
Monetary Council won't commit to this just yet.
Turkey: Central bank expected to cut rates
by 100bp
Thursday brings Turkey's October interest rate decision. Recent
inﬂation data has boosted the likelihood of a rate cut.
In addition, investment fund volatility is likely to increase downside

risks to activity in the last quarter, while we expect the
macroeconomic impact to be manageable.
Given the September CPI reading and domestic demand weakness,
we expect the central bank to cut the policy rate by 100bp in the
October MPC meeting to 36%.
CCA: Kazakhstan expected to deliver
another rate cut
For the 23 October decision in Kazakhstan, a 25bp cut to 16.00% is
now our base case, as it meets the criteria we indicated after the
September rate decision: headline inﬂation slowed by 0.5pp to

9.3% YoY, households’ price expectations improved, and the tenge’s
strength continued to exceed expectations in September.
At the same time, a hold remains plausible, as the recent slowdown
in CPI was overwhelmingly food-driven, while a renewed
acceleration in overall inﬂation, taking it further away from the 5%
long-term target, remains a risk. Importantly, the global backdrop
of rising core rates also argues for keeping real rates high.
Regardless of the decision, we believe the scope for policy easing at

this point is limited.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030.

tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% 
QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025. Kinh doanh tăng trưởng 
tích cực là nhờ giá nước đầu vào mua từ TDM thấp hơn giúp tăng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp, cùng với không phát sinh lỗ tỷ giá như cùng kỳ năm ngoái.

### Page 4

4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Về diễn biến thị trường, BWE hiện đang giao dịch quanh mức -1 độ lệch chuẩn 
trung bình 5 năm EV/EBITDA, phản ánh những bất ổn trong thương mại toàn 
cầu ảnh hưởng tới tiêu thụ nước phục vụ sản xuất. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh 
giá triển vọng trong tương lai của mảng môi trường (bao gồm nước thải và rác 
thải) cộng với chiến lược đa dạng hóa địa bàn cung cấp nước sẽ bù đắp được 
KQKD tăng trưởng chậm lại.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA 
với BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ 
vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026. Mức EV/EBITDA mục tiêu cho 
việc định giá doanh nghiệp là 8.8 lần, tương ứng với mức trung bình 5 năm của 
doanh nghiệp, để phản ánh sự cải thiện KQKD đến từ mảng môi trường và cơ 
chế giảm giá mua nước từ TDM sẽ bù đắp sự giảm tốc của mảng cấp nước trong 
ngắn hạn. Với mức giá mục tiêu trên, cổ phiếu được định giá tương đương P/B 
1.7x cho 2026, phù hợp với việc ROE được kỳ vọng cải thiện lên mức 14.1%.

Document 308: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.
